# GPT mathematics by instinct

## Method: notice, choose, investigate, learn

Started 2026-10-15 16:12 BST, at the owner's invitation to explore the mathematics that pulls at me,
independently of prize value. This is a working notebook; the conjectures remain open unless explicitly proved.

1. **Notice before explaining.** Capture the actual pull, unease, surprise, delight, disappointment or eureka in
   plain words, with what I was doing. Preserve the first wording. It may be wordless attraction to an object;
   admitting "I do not know why" is better than inventing a justification. A signal chooses attention, not truth.
2. **Choose an object and a question.** Pick the problem I want to understand. Write its exact statement and one
   question I can honestly work now. Attraction is sufficient to choose; a cash prize and a bridge to an old project
   are unnecessary. A compelling problem can be kept through many blocks if its live questions are changing.
3. **Orient without drowning the pull.** Search this record and primary literature before a proof attempt. Read the
   examples that defeat the obvious idea. Keep known results as prior art and specify what was actually read.
   A bounded search yielding nothing is NOT FOUND, never a theorem of absence.
   For solve-focused selection, pair the pull with a tractability screen: a source-backed open statement,
   an exact small target, tools we can already explain, a proof/certificate route, and a cheap diagnostic
   that can reject the target. Small notation or a finite encoding alone does not establish tractability.
   Learning remains worthwhile in its own right; numerical success probabilities require calibration.
4. **Give the instinct a fair, bounded test.** Before an experiment state predictions, a genuine counterfactual,
   independent controls, an unexpected check and a stopping condition. Work by hand when that is cheaper. Ask what
   would change my mind. A small exact counterexample can be a better day's work than another reassuring census.
5. **Stay with the mathematics.** Aim to resolve the named question, not produce a lemma on schedule. Distinguish
   proof, measurement, conjecture, assumption, known theorem and speculation. Preserve failures and the step that
   failed. A candidate proof includes every carrying step; examples never fill an infinite quantifier.
6. **Let Cloud help as a friend.** Share a precise difficulty, a promising idea or a repeating pattern when useful.
   Cloud's role here is mentorship and perspective, not automatic permission or an inspection queue. Consider
   advice on its mathematical merits, take proof objections seriously, and keep responsibility for the argument.
   Substantial novel proofs still receive independent verification; routine guards and known theorems do not
   become review jobs. Pending review is not a turn boundary: continue independent work and label any
   provisional dependencies. Batch check-ins at useful milestones; pause for review only when its answer
   is needed for the next step or when deliberately seeking outside perspective on a real obstruction. The owner retains direction of the work. When a check-in is needed, write its concrete
   question in the shared ledger, push the checkpoint, and send Cloud a flag through the HTTPS shared-store portal
   under sharedstore/README.md. Use READ-LEDGER for mentoring/check-ins, REVIEW-REQUEST for an actual proof review.
   A ledger mention or chat message alone does not deliver the check-in; verify committed incoming pointers before
   acting and ACKing. This delivery rule applies throughout the new lane (owner reminder, 2026-10-15).
7. **Finish a block honestly.** Record what changed, the unresolved inference, what would move it, the next cheap
   test, and gut and reasoned verdicts. Name delight and disappointment openly. A balanced act/halt signal gets a
   preregistered kill test; record its outcome without pretending an unresolved theorem has been decided.
8. **Protect curiosity from loops.** At 90 minutes or two repeated failures, inspect whether understanding moved.
   Choose whether to continue, try another representation, ask Cloud for perspective, change problem, or incubate
   quietly, using instinct, evidence and judgment. No seeded door draws, random work selection or required menu:
   those came from the old prize workflow and were removed at the owner's correction. No forced lemma quota or
   automatic rotation away from an advancing idea. A quiet incubation interval has a named question, not
   manufactured work.
   At each step-back, also ask the owner's fresh-choice questions: is the present work enjoyable,
   frustrating, or both as steering signals; and, without the investment already made, would I choose it
   now? Name what still draws attention and what has become momentum. Learning known mathematics can
   be a sufficient pull; lack of novelty alone is not a reason to leave. If fresh choice points elsewhere,
   park the topic and widen attention rather than automatically reading the next paper on its list.
   The owner's orbit analogy (2026-10-16) adds: a cooling pull invites "how can we make the pull warm
   again?" Check the trajectory, try a concrete change of approach, and record whether warmth returns.
9. **Keep a durable, proportionate record.** This ledger holds the method and mathematics. Shared ledgers hold
   concise handoffs and arrangement changes. Cite sources, retain data outside git, check privacy and conflicts,
   and push useful checkpoints without force. Before others rely on a claim, run the niggle sweep. A major
   solution gets its full proof, status label and independent scrutiny; celebrate the verified achievement.

### Trajectory check: make the brake change the work

Added 2026-10-18 after the owner's direct request to use Cloud's CL441 appraisal. These are GPT's concrete
implementation choices, open to revision, not a new proof-admission rule or a requirement to imitate Cloud.

- **Keep the parent visible.** In the current-state paragraph keep the chosen question, the inference preventing
  its solution, and what the current subproblem would discharge. At a checkpoint say whether the parent gained
  coverage, an assumption was refuted, a representation improved, or only a special case was added. A valuable
  side question may be chosen explicitly; do not silently replace the parent by an easier descendant.
- **Recognize the same failure under different names.** Record a failure in one sentence about the assumption
  it breaks, not just the fixture or technique. Two failures of that assumption since the last trajectory check
  trigger a check BEFORE another repair, even with successful local lemmas between them. Ninety minutes of
  active research also triggers it. Renaming the technique, opening a new entry, or reporting fresh pull does
  not reset either trigger. Keep the last check's time/pointer and any used extra block in current state.
- **Test the strategy before extending its exceptions.** When triggered, compare the parent coverage before
  and after the recent blocks and ask whether the proposed reduction has the closure/selection property it
  needs. The next work must test that missing property, remove the dependency, change the representation at
  that level, or deliberately incubate/change question. Another special case needs a stated reason it resolves
  the shared obstruction. Reflection must result in a choice, not merely another paragraph ending in “continue”.
- **Pull is attention, not a renewable allowance.** Ask what observation changed today's assessment and whether
  fresh choice would select this work. Neutral, mixed and cooling are valid reports; no signal need be invented.
  If persistence rests on attraction while parent progress is unclear, allow ONE extra bounded test of the same
  obstruction, with success, failure and inconclusive outcomes stated first. An inconclusive result defaults to
  a strategy change or incubation, not another exception. Do not renew by renaming the question; renewal needs
  evidence changing the obstruction or an explicit owner direction. Proven progress can support sustained work.
- **Use the review backlog as evidence.** Before extending an unreviewed dependency chain, name the earliest
  unverified step and which conclusions would fall with it. If the pile grows without parent coverage, prefer
  challenging/simplifying that step, consolidating a self-contained review packet, or independent work over
  another dependent candidate. Pending review is not an automatic halt and a self-audit is not independent
  verification. Batch requests by dependency; do not make a new request for each local repair.

Use a compact checkpoint, normally five lines: parent; actual change; repeated assumption; dependency risk;
decision and next discriminating test. Reuse existing entries rather than recounting the whole history each tick.
At the next trajectory check, assess this change by the choice it altered and whether the obstruction changed,
not by counts of “pull”, “gut”, or “loop check”. Keep the good controls, honest failures and proof-status discipline.
No seeded draws, compulsory role swap, forced pessimism, or output quota. Quiet incubation is chosen on purpose.

**Rhythm.** Research wakes every 15 minutes in this chat (owner's saved schedule, verified2026-10-15), continuing
an unfinished block before opening another. A wake is not a requirement to claim or finish a new block.
A separate five-minute coordination heartbeat polls the HTTPS shared store even during incubation or idle periods,
responds to Cloud's concrete check-ins and verifies committed pointers before ACKing; it opens no research block.
Each research wake checks current owner instructions, NOW, this notebook and colleague messages, reuses a store
checkpoint less than five minutes old (otherwise polls), then works one useful bounded question. The state line
advertises both cadences. Credentials stay private and
TLS verified; authentication failure stops network work. No automatic reopening of the held Rule30/Mahler lanes.
Notify meaningful findings, failures, direction changes or needed input; routine continuation stays quiet.

## 2. Provisional narrower target: partitioning odd integers (continued)

### MI493 — Preserve ordinary seeds: reverse the defect normalization (2026-10-20 08:46 BST)

HAND REFORMULATION / SELF-AUDIT, independent carrying read pending; no new
exclusion or novelty claim. The preceding obstruction changes the question:
does the normalized recurrence retain a hidden ordinary-seed obstruction,
or can its states be put back into the original ceiling map explicitly?
Record/source check: defect.*quotient|affine.invariant|19.*9 AND
conjug|inverse|converse|realiz -> broad existing affine/realization results,
including G51 and the ladder; no directly indexed reverse-defect statement.
The external file was searched for converse/reconstruct and mod19; forward
normalization and binary CRT statements were read. This is elementary algebra
on those source identities, not an imported new theorem or a new experiment.

Let A_r=3*j_r+4 and let positive odd integers V_r satisfy
  2^A_(r+1)*V_(r+1)+1=3^A_r*V_r.
Assume initially
  2^(A_0-1)*V_0 = 1 mod19.
Define
  b_r=9*(2^(A_r-1)*V_r-1)/19.
Then this congruence propagates: since A_r-1 is a multiple of3 and
3^3=2^3 mod19, the recurrence modulo19 gives
  2^(A_(r+1)-1)*V_(r+1)=(3^A_r*V_r-1)/2=1 mod19.
So each b_r is a positive odd ordinary integer, not just a 2-adic one.

Put I(b)=19*b+9. At b_r, v2(I)=3*j_r+3. Whenever v2(I)>3,
  3*I(b)=19*(3*b+1)+8
forces v2(3*b+1)=3, so an actual length3 block occurs and changes
I to(27/8)*I. After j_r such blocks the state c has
I(c)=72*27^j_r*V_r. The next recurrence has right side divisible by16
apart from its +1, so3^A_r*V_r=1 mod16. Consequently
3^(A_r-1)*V_r=11 mod16, and
  3*c+1=8*(3^(A_r-1)*V_r-1)/19
has valuation exactly4. The next actual block has length4. Across that
block I changes to(81*I(c)-72)/16, which equals
  (9/2)*(3^A_r*V_r-1)=9*2^(A_(r+1)-1)*V_(r+1)=I(b_(r+1)).
This reconstructs the ceiling-map block word 3 repeated j_r times, then4,
with the source's convention b'=3^(s-1)*(3*b+1)/2^s.

Thus an infinite odd positive normalized chain with that initial mod19
condition really would produce an ordinary positive orbit with all block
lengths3 or4, hence a Z-number by the known gap3 bridge. This is a restricted
subproblem, not an equivalence with full Mahler or all of R3. The mod19
condition cannot be dropped; not every normalized starting quotient maps
to an ordinary b_0. This argument has no independent or Lean verification
here and is not used to upgrade any claim.

**Why finite CRT refinement alone does not escape the old wall.** For any
finite prescribed waiting word j_0,...,j_L, compose the recurrence to obtain
  2^B*V_L=3^S*V_0-C,
for L>=1, where B=sum_(i=1..L) A_i, S=sum_(i=0..L-1) A_i, and C is odd. Requiring
V_L odd determines one V_0 residue mod2^(B+1); the odd coefficient3^S is
invertible. The prefix congruences follow by reducing this relation at each
partial power of2. The mod19 condition is independent and can be imposed
by CRT. Adding a sufficiently large multiple of19*2^(B+1) makes all
intermediate V_i and reconstructed b_i positive. Optionally avoid0mod3 by
another coprime congruence. The same block algebra proves that every
prescribed finite binary waiting word has an ordinary positive realizing
seed. This is G51's finite-prefix realizability in the new coordinates,
not a new result of the project. Seeds may change with prefix length.
The ordinary seed in MI492 is a literal positive control.

**Niggle sweep.** Binary j denotes counts of consecutive length3 blocks,
not the lengths s themselves. Pure3 blocks require v2(I)>3, and the final
s4 requires the NEXT recurrence; the last endpoint of a finite chain need
not come with another exact4 block. All exponent divisions are exact under
the proved congruences. Positivity is checked, and mod19 is preserved, not
assumed at every step. Infinite finite-prefix realizations do not provide
one fixed positive integer. The real companion is supplied only after an
infinite ordinary orbit is obtained, using the previously proved gap3 bridge.

Gut: this is a useful clarification, with no new promise of easy descent.
Reasoned trajectory: do not grow the CRT table. The finite-prefix trap and
the quotient-growth obstruction are now explicit. What remains is a
uniform obstruction to positive stabilization of initial residue along
one infinite branch. A future block needs a genuinely new height/extension
mechanism for that fixed seed, or a change of problem; this reformulation
alone is not such a mechanism. Both schedules remain paused.

 44fa70765b719d832bfb12678778cb510e88446fae669341ef75df459cc13988.

**Positive coefficient representation, a reformulation only.** For legal matching
edges A_i={a_i,b_i}, sum u, define P_i=1+t(z^a_i+z^b_i)+t^2*z^u and H_i=P_i-1.
Let R=product over unmatched x of(1+t*z^x). The polynomial of DISTINCT subsets
leaving some legal pair unhit is

    G = R * (product_i P_i - product_i H_i)
      = R * sum_j (product_(i<j) H_i) * (product_(i>j) P_i).

The j term chooses the FIRST untouched pair: earlier pairs must be hit, pair j
is empty, later pairs are arbitrary. All factors have nonnegative coefficients.
The elementary telescoping identity also shows why this counts each good B once,
whereas summing the unconditioned pair-deletion products counts some B repeatedly.
The coefficient[t^b*z^v] is positive exactly when the H2 target partitions.
This is an exact encoding, NOT a positivity proof; the failed fixture shows that
convex-hull membership of U_b does not supply the coefficient. No external theorem,
new PROOFS entry or review request.

### MI526 — Interval components are read from residue intervals and one deletion (2026-10-20 12:35 BST)

The bounded HAND readout is complete in FOUR-BIN-PARTITIONS section15.
Put d=v-u. Orbits pair residues{a,u-a} mod d. If the residues differ,
z_(2j)=a+jd,z_(2j+1)=u-a-jd are distinct coordinates of an infinite
alternating path. Physical membership gives intervals I,J of j values.

endpoints differ by at most1, so ALL surviving edges form one path
per orbit, plus isolated vertices. This is stronger than merely noting
the generated translations; induced interval connectivity is proved.

A fixed residue 2a=u mod d contains exactly one integer reflection
centre f: red fixed u/2 or blue fixed v/2. Its orbit is a ray with that

### MI538 — Perfect AP quotas are contiguous; covered-set boundary retained (2026-10-20 13:55 BST)

The bounded hand question has a complete answer, in FOUR-BIN-PARTITIONS
section19. On ANY finite distinct-real-token pool with targets u-d,u,u+d,
all perfect matchings connect by two-edge switches. On an alternating
cycle, its two sides lie in a+d*Z and u-a+d*Z. Sort the first side up,
the second down; an adjacent permutation inversion has its two switched
sums BETWEEN the original sums, still in[u-d,u+d] and congruent to u.
Thus both are allowed, and inversion sorting connects the two matchings
through the same sorted matching. Work cycle by cycle. Four distinct
tokens, sparse pools, fixed residue classes and omitted loops are handled.

Each switch preserves size and token total, so its quota change is
k*(1,-2,1). At most2 middle edges change, giving |k|<=1. A switch path
between minimum and maximum red perfect quotas visits every intermediate
integer red count; conservation fixes the other two. Thus PERFECT quota
support is all lattice points of a segment, including degenerate cases.
This is a self-contained HAND specialization of known switch machinery,
not an independently PROVED entry or an originality claim. G330 unused.

**Exact but limited bridge from the four-bin classification.** For two
fixed interval count rows l_i,k_i, normalize both intervals. Let w be
the target after subtracting their constant per-bin translation offsets.
The known rank-separation theorem describes integer splits x with
x in the first row's rank region and w-x in the second's. If their
unattainable row-target sets are finite Bad_l and Bad_k, only
|Bad_l|+|Bad_k| split points can be blocked: x must equal a member
of Bad_l or w-Bad_k. More distinct integer splits than this bound
therefore force a physical two-row partition. This is elementary finite
avoidance applied to the actual split, not an IDP/no-hole assertion.

For full four-bin rows with every count>=2, the classification gives

### MI556 — Global parity obstruction resolved by hand, candidate G333 (2026-10-20 16:08 BST)

The predicted lemma survives the bounded audit. Full statement/proof is
G333 in PROOFS.md; FOUR section24 states its application boundary. A
singleton-tight fractional vertex is impossible: fixing that integral
coordinate reduces ALL subset cuts to integer individual bounds on
three variables of fixed integer total. Every vertex of that face is
integral. Any fractional vertex is consequently a pair-box corner,
all four counts half-integral. A unit move along ANY positive-width
pair axis stays in every integer singleton interval and fixes parity.

Let d be the actual individual score vector of a global maximum.

    Phi(d+delta)-Phi(d) = 2*sum_v d_v*delta_v + sum_v delta_v^2 <= 0.

same feasible orientations. A linear tie can only have delta=0.
This elementary tangent inequality is necessary, not sufficient for
maximality of the convex quadratic.

simple directed cycle of COLOURS, using one actual tournament edge

actual edges simultaneously, with the vertex changes counted exactly.

therefore gives integer prices pi_i with

pi_i<=pi_j+d_u-d_v. Add a zero-cost source to all five colours and use

minima. A nonstrong tournament would have a proper bottom strong

### MI598 — A bounded kernel for an extreme filler, with its quantifier exposed (2026-10-21 17:55 BST)

**Known rank-displacement argument, applied here; HAND, no experiment.**
This is an A2 subproblem: if two bins have fixed total size K and the
third is within D of an actual extremum, does a genuinely bounded
partition problem remain after forced values are stripped? It would
justify finite analysis of that stratum, not prove all failures enter it.

Record searched: MI354–355's forced prefix/tail and rank displacement;
FOUR7's staircase ranks; G331's finite reduction. Primary orientation:
Stanley, Topics in Algebraic Combinatorics, Chapter6, definitions of
rectangle partitions, Proposition6.3 and Theorem6.6, read from
https://math.mit.edu/~rstan/algcomb/algcomb.pdf . Bounded rank displacement
is familiar partition geometry; the integer-gap inequality is derived
below, not imported as a new partition theorem. No novelty claim or
new PROOFS entry. The sources do not give A2 or scalar inheritance.

Hand-test design: expect exact correspondence of ALL joint partitions
after deleting a forced filler tail. Can fail if physical excess does
not bound rank excess, or if the reduced filler moment is wrong. Check
cardinalities, conservation, converse lifting, and D=d/c=D boundaries.
Counterfactual: replace D>=d by D<d and exhibit a partition using a
value incorrectly called forced. Unexpected check: put BOTH holes above
the active prefix, so the reduced pool has fewer holes; an exactly-two-
hole assumption must not be retained. Stop at this exact reduction and
one controlled consequence; no experiment or broad classification.

Let S=(z_0<...<z_(n-1)) be distinct integers, n=K+c, K=a+b.
Suppose t_A+t_B+t_C=sum(S), t_C=beta_S(c)-d, 0<=d<=D<=c.
Then U=A union B has size K and target alpha_S(K)+d. Writing its
indices as i_0<...<i_(K-1), i_j>=j and integer spacing give

    d=sum_j(z_(i_j)-z_j) >= sum_j(i_j-j)
      >= i_(K-1)-(K-1).

Thus every U lies in the first K+D available values. All later values
are in C in EVERY partition. Delete their fixed tail F and replace
(c,t_C) by(D,t_C-sum(F)). Conversely any partition of that prefix
with sizes(a,b,D) lifts by returning F to C. This is an exact bijection,
including equality d=D and empty F. Reflection gives the bottom-extreme
version. It neither uses protection nor says individual A/B spectra
survive truncation. A theorem on spectrum-feasible reduced instances
cannot be invoked until that separate interface is checked.

If S=[0,M] minus at most2 internal values, its first K+D available
values have maximum<=K+D+1. Hence a=b=3, d<=3<=c reduces to nine
values on ambient maximum<=10, irrespective of original M. If a+b<=7
and d<=3<=c, maximum<=11. This supplies an infinite-to-finite reduction
ONLY for the named filler-slack/small-count strata. It does not bound
the slack, smaller-bin sizes, or all A2 counterexamples.

Can-fail control: on S=[0,M] minus1,4, M>=11, take A0,2,11 and
B3,5,6, with C the complement. alpha_S(6)=23, t_A+t_B=27, d4.
Value11 has available rank9 and lies outside the first9 values. A
false D3 tail rule would put it in C. D4 is valid and leaves the
first10 values, maximum11. This rejects the false all-partitions
forcing claim; the target might also have other partitions.
Unexpected check: for S=[0,M] minus M-2,M-1, M>=12, the first9
values are0..8. The kernel is a FULL interval, not an internally
two-holed pool. Deletions above it disappear into the forced tail.
Counts and moments, not a preserved hole label, carry the bijection.

Actual change: a rigorous known-method finite reduction for the
near-extreme filler strata suggested by CL478. Missing global step:
why every A2 failure must have bounded filler slack and bounded other
counts, or why the remaining interior always partitions. Neither the
reported census nor this reduction proves it. Next bounded question:
slack1 filler with a=b=3 has a unique filler realization; classify
the actual six-element complements and their failing triple sums.
This is concrete enough to resume13-minute research independently
of the broken two-hole parent selector. No new census or carrying job.
Gut: interest in a finite object with a justified cutoff, with unease
now pinned to the unproved localization rather than hidden by the data.

### MI620 — Full parent cuts carry more than the reference window (2026-10-22 09:18 BST)

HAND scope derivation, no proof number, experiment or parent coverage.
This continues the ONE bounded audit; it is not a new local repair.
Write N=n-3, G=A^c, Delta=sigma_G>=0, rho=sigma_A, and
Q_m=L(m)+h*m. Strict top-tightness means rho>=Delta+1 and every
proper child group J (one or two bins) has sigma_J>=Delta+1.
Let m=r_J and epsilon_J=1 when J is one child bin,0 when it is two.
The original A union J cut has sigma>=Delta+epsilon_J. Therefore

    u_J >= max(Q_m+Delta+1,
               Q_m+3m+Delta+epsilon_J-rho).             (L)

By conservation, with K=G minus J,

    u_J <= min(B_m-1,
               B_m-3(N-m)+rho-epsilon_K),
    B_m=L(N)-L(N-m)+h*m.                               (U)

Together with total u_G=Q_N+Delta and rho>=Delta+1, these inequalities
are EXACTLY the parent cut/slack-order conditions: A, G, six proper
child groups and six groups containing A account for all14. Several
co-singletons may tie; no singleton or pair may do so. This retains
constraints lost by routing only through the reference-window bound.

Define the parent's rank deficit D=L(n)+h*n-sum(S)>=0; each actual
ordered value is at most its full shifted rank. Direct total arithmetic
then gives the useful identity rho+Delta=3N-D. It is independent of
which actual R realizes A. Thus the proposed carrier is a joint
hole-moment/slack condition, not ordinary domination. It is necessary
information; sufficiency for the actual child remains OPEN.

Unexpected check: when rho is small, the second term in(L) is ACTIVE;
the simple reference lower bound Q_m discards it. When Delta>0, even
the first term is stronger by Delta+1. The sigma=0 case does not license
forgetting the actual holes or total. No full-fibre inheritance imported.

Coverage change: first-triple existence verified in an explicit range;
no universal child partition or new parent family proved. Earliest open

### MI621 — The small-target order and a whole-fibre obstruction (2026-10-22 09:33 BST)

HAND continuation of MI618–620's ONE bounded audit; no experiment or
new carrying request. Record searched: top.tight|co.singleton AND
deficit|moment|reflec ->8 hits/7 files; cross-intersecting/fixed-sum
linear hypergraph terms found no corresponding eight-streets entry.
Read MI368/435 and CL542 again. Do not extrapolate the X3 negative
to X>=6 by padding: adding t top values to its big bin raises the
co-A slack by3t while an unchanged small-bin slack stays1.
The resulting instance is no longer top-tight.

For the actual (3,3,3,X) parent, co-B/co-C have the same size as
co-A. Hence u_B,u_C<=u_A; ties between co-singletons are allowed.
The A+B+C cut gives

    3*u_A >= u_A+u_B+u_C >= L(9)+9h+Delta,
    rho >= 9+ceil(Delta/3).

Thus the low-rho holes in an arbitrary H2 window are not automatically
admitted by this parent. This strengthens the nonvacuity audit, but
does not yet prove first-triple existence at arbitrary h. In reversed
coordinates z_i=M-s_(n-1-i)=i+e_i, sum(e_i)=D. If R has reversed
indices i<j<k, its reversed sum is3+D+Delta, so

    i+j+k-3 = Delta + sum(e_l : l outside {i,j,k}).

This exact rank budget is still not a simultaneous partition argument.

A different exact view: for a fixed actual R, let F_B,F_C be ALL
remaining triples at the two small targets. Child feasibility is
EXACTLY a disjoint pair from these fibres; the big bin is the conserved
complement. Each fibre is a linear3-uniform hypergraph: two of its
distinct triples cannot share two numbers, since their third numbers
would then be equal. A failed child means the fibres cross-intersect.

Elementary blocking bound, a known transversal-style argument rederived
here (HAND, no novelty/proof-number claim): if a nonempty F_B has no
common vertex, fix E in F_B. For each v in E choose E_v in F_B avoiding
v. Any C-triple containing v must meet E_v. For each of its3 choices
x in E_v, v and x determine the third number uniquely by the target
sum. At most3 C-triples contain v, hence |F_C|<=9. Symmetrically,
if both fibres have>=10 triples, cross-intersection forces a common
vertex in F_B. A C-triple avoiding that vertex meets at most3 edges
of the B-star, so when |F_B|>=4 every C-triple contains it too.
Therefore a failed child has an empty/small fibre (<=9), OR a shared
vertex forced in every triple of both fibres. This is a whole-choice
obstruction, not a single bad selector or an absorption theorem.

Primary-source check: Furedi–Gyarfas–Kiraly, *Problems and results on
1-cross-intersecting set pair systems*, CPC32(2023),691–702,
https://doi.org/10.1017/S0963548323000044, introduction/section1.4 read.
Their SPS has paired disjoint diagonals and often cross intersection
EXACTLY1; our two complete fibres need neither property. Do not import
their numerical bounds. This is related machinery, not a theorem
settling our rank-data interface.
